FOOTBALL. LANCASHIRE LEAGUE.

Earlestown v. Stockport County.

The above match under the auspices of the Lancas- hire League was played at Earlestown on Saturday in dull and rather misty weather. The visitors won the toss and decided to kick uphill in the first half, Mr. C W Ealnutt setting the sphere in moti-bn on behalf of Banes- town before about 1,000 spectators. Several cnanges were made from the advertised team for Earlestown, Hoses being unable to play owing to an injury he re- ceived to his foot on Friday afternoon. This necessitated Whitshouse dropping into tin, intermrdia.te line and Green taking Hoye& place at full back,Harrison playing inside right instead of Whitehouse. Perhaps the change will be seen better by the following list. Eirlestown : Chew, goal; Green, and Cottam backs; Pennington. Whitehouse and Johnson, half-backs; Barnes, Harrison, Hampson, Leach and Slttle, for- wards.

Stockport County : Moore, goal; Harding and Wainwri.qbt, backs: Hall, Yates, and Harvey. half-backs; Foster. Parker, Patterson, Cnesworth and Betteriey, forwards.

The oprning stages qv ere chiefly in favour of the County, their forwards seeming to adapt themselves to the heavy state of the ground better than the home quintette. However' after a while the Earlestown de- f, ace prevailed and Settle and Leach were soon c3rier- ing down the wing, a fine shot from the latter skimming the crossbar. From the ensuing goal kick Focer obtained possession and easily outdistancing his pursuers he put in a fine centre which fell at the feet of Patterson who just tapped the bell, and Stockport were one in front. It was a well worked for point, the home defence being' powerless, as there were four of the County forwards behind each other waiting for Foster's centre, and when it did come Chew bad no possible chance of paving. This was all the scoring done up to the interval, when the county were leading by ono to nil.

Restarting,the homesters took up the running,Birnes mulling a splendid chance of equalising the scores. A spell of midfield play followed for a while, the proceed- ings being at length enlivened by a pretty piece of pass- ing between Settle and Leach, the latter eventually cleverly evading Harding and passing the !either to Hampson who shot hard and straight at Moore, the custodian just tipping the ball over the bar. The ensu- ing corner was beautifully placed by Leach, the ball curling in and passing into the net without being touched by a second player. End to end play followed, both goals being visited in turn. Before the finish, Setterley scored a second ar.d Cheswcrtb a third goal for the County who were eventually declared the winners of a eosely contest° game by three goals to nil.

At the interval the vinifem were leadlng by three to nil.—ln the second half Earlestown were content to rest on their laurels and act on the de- fensive which they did with a marked degree of surce3s as their goal wai kept intact during the whole of the half and when the wistle blew the half-time score was imalt,red. Eirlestown fully deserved to win as they played the better game throughout,the Haydock plasers relying too much on individual work whereas the Earles- town team worked as one combining beautifully through- out.

===== 0002601_18991229_art0160.txt | included=true | included =====
South Liverpool v, Earlestown.

This league match was played at Shorefields on Tues- day afternoon before a goodly crowd of enthusiasts. South Liverpool kicked off, and wore early aggressive ; and after s mus smart forward work opened the score through the agency of Bennett. This reverse was the means of livening up the play of the Earlestown team ; and the game became fa,t, the scone of hostilities being located in home territory. Several smart shots were sent in by Bird, none of which, however, reached the desired mark, and the ball was taken to the other end. Play did not remain there long; and Earlestown showed clever tactics, several time, having hard luck. Tbe South-enders' front rank then got away, and Chew't: charge was subjected to a hot fusilade of shots, the colours of the Earlestown contingent being lowered for the second time during a bully in the goahnouth. There was no holding the home team after this, and they kept up a strong attack upon the Earlestown citadel, which had several narrow shaves; but the backs were pretty safe, and no further score was registered. At balf-thne the game rood : South, Liverpool, 1:; Earlestown, nil. Final .—Sauth Liverpool. 3 ; Earlestown, 2.
